### Activities

| Activity | Duration | Adult (¥) | Party (¥) | Location | Details |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Osaka Castle keep](#castles-and-fortifications) | 1:40 | 600 | 1,800 | 1-1 Osakajo, Chuo-ku, Osaka 540-0002 | The keep looks like a wooden Sengoku-era castle from the outside but is not one: Toyotomi Hideyoshi's original 16th-century keep burned in 1615, a Tokugawa-era rebuild burned again in 1665, and what stands today is a 1931 reinforced-concrete reconstruction sitting on the surviving Toyotomi-era stone base. That matters for a visitor expecting a creaky wooden interior like Himeji's - inside is a museum on Hideyoshi and the castle town, plus an 8th-floor observation level, and critically, a lift. That elevator is exactly why this castle works with a stroller-and-carrier family when a genuine Edo-period keep like Himeji's (narrow wooden stairs only) does not. The park grounds are free and open all day; keep admission is paid, infants free, and the stroller is parked at the entrance while the party rides up. |
| [Den Den Town (Nipponbashi/Ota Road)](#anime-manga-and-games) | 0:20 | 0 | 0 | Nipponbashi/Ota Road, Naniwa-ku/Chuo-ku, Osaka (district; no single address) | Osaka's own anime and hobby-shop district, a roughly 1km strip along Sakaisuji and the parallel Ota Road with over 150 shops - Animate, Yellow Submarine, Super Potato, K-Books, and other figure, Gunpla, card and doujinshi stores. It shares some of the same chains as Tokyo's Akihabara, but reads noticeably different in person: smaller, far less crowded, more ground-level shopfronts than Akihabara's cramped multi-floor buildings, and a more retro, collector-hobbyist feel than Akihabara's denser, more mainstream-tourist buzz - genuinely more Kansai than Kanto in atmosphere. Free to browse. Nippombashi Station, which this day already uses to reach Kuromon Ichiba, sits right at the district's edge, so this costs almost no extra transit. Flat, wide shopping street, stroller-workable outside peak hours. |
| [Shinsekai walk + Tsutenkaku Tower observation deck](#views-and-observation-decks) | 0:50 | 900 | 2,700 | 1-18-6 Ebisuhigashi, Naniwa-ku, Osaka 556-0002 | Shinsekai ("New World") is a retro entertainment district built in 1912, deliberately modeled on Paris and New York's Coney Island - radial streets and an amusement-park layout centered on Tsutenkaku Tower. It boomed early, was flattened in WWII bombing, and spent decades after as Osaka's cheap, slightly disreputable corner before becoming today's beloved-but-still-a-bit-seedy tourist draw: gaudy signage, plastic food models, and the city's densest cluster of kushikatsu counters. Tsutenkaku itself is a 103m steel observation tower first built in 1912 and rebuilt in 1956 after wartime dismantling; the deck is elevator-served with an open view over Shinsekai's low rooftops - a smaller, more retro stand-in for Umeda Sky Building or Abeno Harukas, and this trip's "city from above" stop. Infants free on the tower; the surrounding streets are flat and stroller-viable but narrow and crowded at peak hours. |
| [Dotonbori canal walk](#streets-lanes-and-canal-walks) | 0:30 | 0 | 0 | Dotonbori canal, Chuo-ku, Osaka (canal-side pedestrian strip; no single street address) | A packed pedestrian walk along both banks of the Dotonbori canal, wall-to-wall illuminated signage - most famously the Glico running man, a billboard first put up by confectioner Ezaki Glico in 1935 and rebuilt six times since (the current LED version dates to 2014) at the same spot on the Ebisubashi bridge, arguably the single most photographed spot in Osaka. It's gaudy on purpose - restaurant signage competing for foot traffic over ninety years, not a curated attraction - and rewards about an hour at most, best after dark when the signs are lit. Level paved streets, stroller-viable, but expect dense evening crowds on both banks. |
| [Tombori River Cruise](#transport-as-an-experience) | 0:30 | 2,000 | 6,000 | Tazaemonbashi Pier, by the Don Quijote store, Dotonbori, Chuo-ku, Osaka (pier location; no street address) | A 20-minute narrated boat ride along the Dotonbori canal, passing underneath the illuminated signs the party just walked past on foot - the same stretch seen from the water, with a live guide pointing out landmarks. It's a good change of pace after a day on foot, but it runs on a scheduled departure rather than being a walk-up guarantee. Adult fare is ¥2,000, infants free (one free child per fare-paying adult, comfortably inside policy for 2 infants and 3 adults). Boarding is via a short ramp/steps at the pier; the stroller is folded and carried aboard or left curbside with one adult - confirm with staff at boarding. |

**Running total after Day 29: ¥1,175,504 ($7,584)**

**Option on the table, not scheduled: Bunraku at the National Bunraku Theatre.** The theatre sits one minute from Kintetsu Nipponbashi Station, the same block as this day's Kuromon Ichiba lunch stop, and its Kinshu (Autumn) run covers 31 Oct - 23 Nov 2026 - open the entire Osaka stay. Bunraku puppets are worked in full view, roughly a metre tall, each handled by three visible black-clad puppeteers, with a chanter and shamisen player performing live at the side of the stage; nothing is spoken that needs translating to follow it, which makes it arguably the single best answer on this whole trip to "how is Japanese theatre different from American theatre." Tickets run about ¥6,500/adult, uniform seating, and the theatre's own FAQ sets no minimum age - only a policy that a disruptive child may be asked to step out, with lobby monitors as a fallback. **It is not scheduled, for one plain reason: Part 1 alone runs 2h20 or longer, which means one adult sits alone with both infants for that entire stretch on the trip's last full day**, and taking it means dropping the Tombori River Cruise above and trimming the Shinsekai stop to make room. It is priced and ready to book as-is if the family decides that trade is worth it; it is left out of the schedule and the Day 29 Cost table above on the assumption that it is not.

## Day 30 - Thursday, November 5, 2026 - Osaka -> Kansai Airport (KIX)

**Base:** [Osaka, departing](#osaka)  
**Weather (typical):** 17°C / 10°C, dry  
**Theme:** Departure - Namba to Kansai Airport on the Nankai line, buffered for international check-in with a party of five

The **JR Kansai-Hiroshima Area Pass expired at the end of Day 28 (Nov 3)**, so today's fare is paid in full. The **Nankai Airport Line rapid express** (¥930/adult, ¥2,790 for the party, ~45 min) is used rather than the JR Haruka, which runs roughly double that per adult for the same trip - Nankai is both cheaper and requires no reservation. International check-in at KIX wants about 3 hours ahead of departure for a party this size with a stroller and checked bags, so that buffer is built into the schedule below rather than assumed.

### Schedule

| Time | Duration | Type | Item |
|---|---|---|---|
| 07:30 | 0:45 | [Food](#meals-29) | Final breakfast buffet at hotel (included) |
| 08:15 | 0:45 | Admin | Pack up, hotel checkout |
| 09:00 | 0:15 | [Transit](#transport-29) | Walk hotel -> Nankai Namba Stn (stroller + bags) |
| 09:15 | 0:45 | [Transit](#transport-29) | Namba Stn -> Kansai Airport Stn (Nankai Airport Line, rapid express) |
| 10:00 | 0:15 | [Transit](#transport-29) | Arrive KIX, walk to check-in hall (Terminal 1) |
| 10:15 | 2:45 | Admin | International check-in, bag drop, security, immigration - 3h buffer for party of 5 + stroller + checked bags; stroller gate-checked at boarding |
| 13:00 | 0:30 | [Food](#meals-29) | Light meal at airside food court before boarding |
| 13:30 | - | Admin | Board flight (airfare and in-flight meal not costed in this itinerary) |

Active out-of-lodging time: about 4h30, with no midday rest block needed - a short, buffered travel day to the airport, comfortably under the ~7h guideline.

### Lodging

No lodging charge - checked out this morning. (Night 3 of 3 at Toyoko Inn Osaka Namba was Day 29's night; Day 30 has no additional room night.)

### Meals

| Meal | What / Where | Address or store | kcal/adult | Cost (¥) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Breakfast | Toyoko Inn buffet, final morning: rice, miso soup, natto, pickles, sliced bread, coffee | Toyoko Inn Osaka Namba | 500 | 0 (included) |
| Lunch | Light pre-flight meal - a bowl of [ramen](#noodles) or Japanese curry rice at an airside food court, timed before boarding an international flight rather than relying on airline catering | Kansai International Airport, Terminal 1, 1 Senshu Kuko Kita, Izumisano, Osaka 549-0001 | 700 | 3,600 |
| Dinner | In-flight meal, international carrier (not costed in this itinerary) | - | 750 | 0 |
| Infant food | Formula (Wakodo or Pigeon) and Morinaga pouches for Infant A packed for the flight; Infant B eats off the airport meal and packed snacks - soft rice, banana, crackers | - | n/a | 1,400 |

Adult kcal/day: 500 + 700 + 750 = **1,950**.

### Transport

| Leg | Mode | Duration | Adult fare (¥) | Party cost (¥) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hotel -> Nankai Namba Stn | Walk | 0:15 | 0 | 0 |
| Namba Stn -> Kansai Airport Stn | [Nankai Airport Line, rapid express](#3-airport-transfers) | 0:45 | 930 | 2,790 |

### Activities

*(No activities scheduled - departure day.)*

### Packing and departure notes

**What must be consumed, binned or left behind before checkout (08:15) and before security:**
- Any opened pouch, snack packet or half-finished formula tin from the Day 27 Osaka run - the
 run was sized to run out around Day 29 night, so there should be little to nothing left; bin
 what remains rather than carry an open, mostly-empty tin through security.
- Any part-used nappy pack: keep only what's needed for the travel day itself (flight plus
 transit) in hand luggage; leave a genuinely spare handful behind rather than repack a torn bag.
- Hotel toiletries, opened wipes packets not needed in-flight, anything not going in the carry-on
 or the checked bag - leave for housekeeping rather than binning usable items.
- **The nappy stock should run out almost exactly here.** The pack bought in Hiroshima on Day 24
 was sized to cover Hiroshima's four nights plus Osaka's three, and today is the seventh. If a
 handful are left they go straight into hand luggage for the flight; a torn part-pack is not
 worth repacking.

**What's prohibited or restricted in hand luggage on an international departure from KIX:**
- The standard liquids/gels rule applies: containers over 100ml are not allowed in the cabin
 outside of a single clear 1L resealable bag, and that bag's total liquid volume is capped
 (typically ~1L) at most international airports, KIX included.
- **Baby formula, breast milk and baby food (including pouches/purees) typically carry a
 medical or infant exemption** to the 100ml liquids rule in most jurisdictions, including Japan
 and most international carriers' home countries, when a child is travelling with the party.
 This usually means these items can exceed 100ml but must be declared and presented separately
 at the screening tray, and may be tested.
- Formula **powder** (an unopened tin, or any that's left) is not a liquid and is not subject to
 the liquids rule at all - it can travel in carry-on or checked luggage without restriction.
- Nappies, wipes and a few days of dry pouches/snacks are fine in carry-on with no special
 handling needed.

## Osaka + departure segment totals (Days 28-30)

| Category | ¥ | $ |
|---|---|---|
| Lodging | 26,000 | 168 |
| Food | 25,200 | 163 |
| Transport | 7,200 | 46 |
| Activities | 21,750 | 140 |
| **Segment total** | **80,150** | **517** |

**Confirmed against the envelope** (Food ¥25,000 / Transport ¥10,300 / Activities ¥18,000):
- Food: ¥25,200 vs ¥25,000 - **+0.8%**, within the ±5% band.
- Transport: ¥7,200 vs ¥10,300 - **-30.1%**, under. Genuine, not padded: the JR Kansai-Hiroshima Area Pass covers all Day 28 JR travel to and from Kobe at ¥0 on its final valid day, Namba/Dotonbori/Shinsekai/Kuromon sit close enough together to cover several connections on foot, and swapping the paid Kitano city-bus fare (¥780 party) for a JR hop to Kobe Harborland that the pass also covers removes another paid leg.
- Activities: ¥21,750 vs ¥18,000 - **+20.8%**, over the ±5% band. Anpanman Children's Museum & Mall (Day 28, ¥11,250 - see note below on why the party cost isn't adult x3) is the main driver, while Day 29 keeps its paid admissions (Osaka Castle keep, Tsutenkaku, the Tombori River Cruise at its verified ¥2,000/adult fare - see below) plus the free Den Den Town add. The overage is the direct, expected cost of the approved Anpanman addition, funded from the trip's discretionary budget per the decision list, not padded or hidden.
- Lodging (fixed, not part of the flex envelope): ¥26,000 for nights 2 and 3 of 3 at Toyoko Inn Osaka Namba, matching the ¥13,000/night rate in the brief.

**On the Anpanman party cost:** unlike almost every other site in this itinerary, the Museum Zone charges everyone aged 1 and up, infants included - both Infant A (~12mo) and Infant B (~20mo) are past that threshold by the Nov 3 visit date, so the ¥11,250 party figure is 5 payers at roughly ¥2,250 each (3 adults + 2 infants), not the usual adult x 3. This is the stated exception to the Party = Adult x 3 rule.

The Transport underspend is not overspend elsewhere - it is real savings from the pass's last day and from routing the Anpanman trip over an already-covered JR leg, left as-is per the honesty rule rather than padded to hit the target.

The Tombori River Cruise appears once across Days 27-30, on Day 29 as the day's closing activity, at the verified market rate of **¥2,000/adult (¥6,000 party), confirmed via osaka-info.jp** - 20 minutes, departing Tazaemonbashi Pier by the Don Quijote store, infants free. Umeda Sky Building is deliberately not added here: it sits in Kita, across town from every other Osaka stop in this file, Day 28 is already at the pacing cap, and Tsutenkaku already covers the "view over the city" beat on Day 29. Osaka Castle appears exactly once across Days 27-30, on Day 29.

# Appendices

Reference material. The topic index is the fastest way in if you want to find
something by subject rather than by date.

## Appendix A: Topic index

This index lists what the itinerary actually contains, organized by subject rather than by date, so a question like "is there a castle, and when" or "where do we eat ramen" can be answered without reading all thirty days. Each row names the day it happens, where it is, and one practical fact - a cost, a booking constraint, or what sets that instance apart from the others on the same list. Costs shown are per adult unless a row says otherwise; both infants ride and enter free almost everywhere except where a row flags an exception.

### Temples and Buddhism

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Senso-ji | [2](#day-2---thursday-october-8-2026---tokyo) | 2-3-1 Asakusa, Taito-ku, Tokyo 111-0032<br>Link: [senso-ji.jp](https://www.senso-ji.jp/) | Tokyo's oldest Buddhist temple, founded in 645 AD and dedicated to Kannon, goddess of mercy, reached along the covered Nakamise-dori shopping street to the huge red Kaminarimon lantern gate, the main hall and a five-story pagoda. A Buddhist temple is marked by a heavier, roofed gate (sanmon) with guardian statues and incense, unlike a Shinto shrine's simple torii - and Senso-ji shows both at once, since Asakusa Shrine sits inside the same grounds. Free to enter, grounds always open, main hall roughly 6am-5pm. Nakamise-dori is too dense for a stroller by mid-morning, so carry rather than push. |
| Zojoji Temple | [4](#day-4---saturday-october-10-2026---tokyo) | 4-7-35 Shibakoen, Minato-ku, Tokyo (address per site research; postal code unverified)<br>Link: [zojoji.or.jp](https://www.zojoji.or.jp/) | The Tokugawa shoguns' family temple, fronted by the towering Sangedatsumon gate and rows of small stone jizo statues in red knit caps. Free, far less crowded than Senso-ji, and stands directly in front of Tokyo Tower for a classic old-gate, old-tower photo. Flat paved grounds throughout, no stairs, fully stroller-friendly. |
| Kotoku-in (Great Buddha) | [7](#day-7---tuesday-october-13-2026---kamakura-day-trip) | 4-2-28 Hase, Kamakura, Kanagawa 248-0016<br>Link: [kotoku-in.jp](https://www.kotoku-in.jp/) | A 13.4m bronze seated Buddha cast in 1252, sitting in the open air since its housing hall was washed away by a tsunami centuries ago - one of Japan's most recognizable single images. ¥300 per adult; the open gravel forecourt is fully stroller-passable, and an optional stairs-only climb into the statue's interior is +¥50. |
| Hase-dera | [7](#day-7---tuesday-october-13-2026---kamakura-day-trip) | Hase, Kamakura, Kanagawa (nearest station Hase, Enoden; address unverified)<br>Link: [hasedera.jp](https://www.hasedera.jp/) | A Buddhist temple famous for a 9.18m gilded wooden statue of the eleven-headed Kannon, plus a hillside garden with sea views over Kamakura and hundreds of small Jizo statues left by grieving parents. ¥400 per adult; the terraced garden is reached by steep stairs the stroller cannot manage, so carrier only, though the lower grounds are flat. |
| To-ji | [15](#day-15---wednesday-october-21-2026---kyoto) | 1 Kujocho, Minami-ku, Kyoto 601-8473<br>Link: [toji.or.jp](https://toji.or.jp/) | Head temple of Shingon Buddhism, free to enter, with a five-story pagoda towering over its open gravel grounds. The Kobo-ichi flea-and-food market, tied to founder Kobo Daishi's death anniversary, fills the grounds on the 21st of every month with roughly 1,000 stalls. Main paths are stroller-workable; the market itself is a tight press of stalls better suited to a carrier. |
| Tenryu-ji | [17](#day-17---friday-october-23-2026---kyoto) | 68 Susukinobaba-cho, Saga Tenryuji, Ukyo-ku, Kyoto 616-8385<br>Link: [tenryuji.com](https://www.tenryuji.com/) | A UNESCO World Heritage Zen temple founded in 1339, head of its own Rinzai-sect lineage. Its Sogenchi Garden, one of Japan's oldest surviving garden designs, is built around a pond and uses the wooded Arashiyama hillside behind it as "borrowed scenery." ¥500 per adult; today's ticket covers the garden only, not the hall interior. Flat gravel loop, stroller-workable but often busy. |
| Kiyomizu-dera | [18](#day-18---saturday-october-24-2026---kyoto) | 1-294 Kiyomizu, Higashiyama-ku, Kyoto 605-0862<br>Link: [kiyomizudera.or.jp](https://www.kiyomizudera.or.jp/) | Founded in 778 and named for a waterfall on the grounds; the current main hall dates to a 1633 rebuild. Its wooden stage juts 13m out over the hillside on cypress pillars joined without nails, giving one of Kyoto's best views over the wooded hillside and city. ¥500 per adult, UNESCO World Heritage; the approach lanes and grounds are uneven worn stone, manageable with a stroller on the main paths but expect to fold it on the steepest stretches. |
| Kinkaku-ji | [19](#day-19---sunday-october-25-2026---kyoto) | 1 Kinkakuji-cho, Kita-ku, Kyoto 603-8361<br>Link: [shokoku-ji.jp](https://www.shokoku-ji.jp/kinkakuji/) | A three-story Zen pavilion with its top two floors entirely gold-leafed, set over a reflecting pond and among Japan's most recognizable images. Built in 1397 as a shogun's retirement villa; the building standing today is a 1955 reconstruction after a novice monk burned the original to the ground in 1950. ¥500 per adult, exterior and garden walk only, no entry into the pavilion; paved path, stroller-workable. |
| Ryoan-ji | [19](#day-19---sunday-october-25-2026---kyoto) | 13 Ryoanji Goryonoshitacho, Ukyo-ku, Kyoto 616-8001<br>Link: [ryoanji.jp](https://www.ryoanji.jp/) | Japan's most famous karesansui, or dry Zen rock garden: raked white gravel with fifteen stones arranged so that from any single point on the viewing veranda at least one stone is always hidden from view. ¥600 per adult; viewed from the temple hall's raised wooden veranda, shoes off at the entrance, so it's a carrier stop rather than a stroller one. |

Grounds and museum are otherwise flat, paved and stroller-friendly. |
| Daisho-in Temple | [25](#day-25---saturday-october-31-2026---miyajima-itsukushima) | 210 Miyajima-cho, Hatsukaichi, Hiroshima 739-0531 (nearest station: Miyajima pier, about 15 min walk)<br>Link: [daisho-in.com](https://daisho-in.com/) | A working Shingon Buddhist mountain temple at the base of Mt. Misen, founded in 806, with an entrance stairway lined with spinning metal sutra wheels each inscribed with a sutra a visitor can "read" just by turning it. The grounds also hold a dim cave lined with 88 icons standing in for the Shikoku pilgrimage and a hillside of hundreds of rakan statues, each with a distinct carved face. Free; the lower grounds are workable but the stepped approach beyond calls for a carrier. |
| Todai-ji Daibutsuden | [20](#day-20---monday-october-26-2026---kyoto-day-trip-to-nara) | 406-1 Zoshi-cho, Nara 630-8211<br>Link: [todaiji.or.jp](https://www.todaiji.or.jp/) | The Great Buddha Hall (Daibutsuden) is one of the largest wooden buildings in the world, even though today's structure is a smaller Edo-period rebuild of a bigger 8th-century original. Inside sits the Daibutsu, a 15m bronze seated Buddha cast in 752 CE, one of the largest bronze Buddha statues on Earth. ¥800 per adult; flat, paved, stroller-friendly paths from the park entrance, though the wild deer along the approach can nip or headbutt at a toddler's eye level. |

The kilometre-long lantern approach is free; the paid inner precinct (Chumon gate and moss garden), ¥500 per adult, has gravel paths slightly uneven enough that a carrier works better than a stroller. |
| Itsukushima Shrine | [25](#day-25---saturday-october-31-2026---miyajima-itsukushima) | 1-1 Miyajima-cho, Hatsukaichi, Hiroshima 739-0588<br>Link: [itsukushimajinja.jp](https://www.itsukushimajinja.jp/) | A Shinto shrine built out over the tidal flats on wooden pillars, founded in the 6th century and rebuilt in its present form in 1168 - Miyajima itself has long been considered sacred ground, historically closed to births and deaths. The famous vermilion torii stands about 200m offshore and appears to float on the water only at high tide; at low tide the sand flats are exposed and you can walk out to its base, so which one a visitor gets depends entirely on the tide table for the day. ¥300 per adult; the raised walkways are flat and stroller-navigable, and an elevator underpass avoids the one stairway. |

| What | Day | Where | What it is |

| Imperial Palace East Gardens | [4](#day-4---saturday-october-10-2026---tokyo) | 1-1 Chiyoda, Chiyoda-ku, Tokyo 100-8111<br>Link: [kunaicho.go.jp](https://www.kunaicho.go.jp/event/higashigyoen/higashigyoen.html) | The public portion of the Imperial Palace grounds - the former Edo Castle's inner citadel, opened as a garden in 1968, with the stone Tenshudai foundation (the burned keep's base) still standing. Free, no admission fee or advance booking needed, same-day entry at the gate; closed Mondays, Fridays, and late December through early January. Flat gravel paths throughout except the Tenshudai's stone stairs, which need a carrier. |
| Nijo Castle | [19](#day-19---sunday-october-25-2026---kyoto) | 541 Nijojo-cho, Nakagyo-ku, Kyoto 604-8301<br>Link: [nijo-jocastle.city.kyoto.lg.jp](https://nijo-jocastle.city.kyoto.lg.jp/) | Built in 1603 as the Kyoto residence of the Tokugawa shogun, Japan's military ruler - a deliberate show of shogunal power inside the emperor's own capital. The Ninomaru Palace's corridors have "nightingale floors," floorboards engineered to chirp underfoot as a low-tech intruder alarm, still audible today. ¥1,300 per adult covers both the grounds/garden and the shoeless, no-photography palace interior; leave the stroller at the entrance for the interior, though the outer grounds are gravel and stroller-workable. |
| Hiroshima Castle | [26](#day-26---sunday-november-1-2026---hiroshima-city-day) | 21-1 Motomachi, Naka-ku, Hiroshima 730-0011<br>Link: [hiroshimacastle.jp](https://hiroshimacastle.jp/) | A five-story castle keep, originally built in the 1590s and destroyed instantly in the 1945 bombing; what had stood since 1958 was a concrete reconstruction closely following the original exterior design, with a small history museum inside. The keep closed permanently on March 22, 2026 for safety, due to concrete deterioration and aging infrastructure, ahead of a planned wooden rebuild - only the exterior, grounds and moat path can be seen on this visit, not the interior museum. Grounds and moat path are flat and fully stroller-workable. |
| Osaka Castle keep | [29](#day-29---wednesday-november-4-2026---osaka) | 1-1 Osakajo, Chuo-ku, Osaka 540-0002<br>Link: [osakacastle.net](https://www.osakacastle.net/) | The keep looks like a wooden Sengoku-era castle from the outside but is not one: Toyotomi Hideyoshi's original 16th-century keep burned in 1615, a Tokugawa-era rebuild burned again in 1665, and what stands today is a 1931 reinforced-concrete reconstruction sitting on the surviving Toyotomi-era stone base. Inside is a museum on Hideyoshi and the castle town, an 8th-floor observation level, and critically a lift, which is why this castle works with a stroller-and-carrier family when a genuine wooden keep like Himeji's does not. ¥600 per adult, infants free; park grounds are free and open all day, the stroller is parked at the entrance while the party rides up. |

### The atomic bombing and war memory

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Atomic Bomb Dome, return visit | [26](#day-26---sunday-november-1-2026---hiroshima-city-day) | 1-10 Otemachi, Naka-ku, Hiroshima 730-0051<br>Link: [peace-tourism.com](https://peace-tourism.com/en/spot/a-bombdome.html) | A slower second look at the same ruin, on the way to the Peace Memorial Museum. The exposed dome frame is structural damage from the blast, not a design feature, and knowing that is the difference between seeing a ruin and understanding what it is. Free, outdoor, along the flat Motoyasu riverside promenade, with benches nearby. |
| Peace Memorial Museum | [26](#day-26---sunday-november-1-2026---hiroshima-city-day) | 1-2 Nakajima-cho, Naka-ku, Hiroshima<br>Link: [hpmmuseum.jp](https://hpmmuseum.jp/?lang=eng) | Hiroshima's central museum on the atomic bombing, built around personal artifacts, photographs and survivor testimony rather than an abstracted overview. The lower-floor galleries are graphic and confronting, showing burned clothing and melted objects without softening the human toll, so the plan splits the party here, one or two adults going through while the other stays outside in the park with the children. ¥200 per adult, free under 18. |
| Hiroshima Orizuru Tower | [26](#day-26---sunday-november-1-2026---hiroshima-city-day) | 1-2-1 Otemachi, Naka-ku, Hiroshima 730-0051<br>Link: [orizurutower.jp](https://www.orizurutower.jp/en/) | A modern observation tower directly across from the Dome, with an open-air rooftop deck over Peace Park and the city, and a spiral ramp down through a glass wall where visitors fold and drop a paper crane into the atrium below. A deliberately lighter, hands-on stop right after the museum. ¥2,200 per adult, elevator access throughout. |
| Port of Kobe Earthquake Memorial Park | [28](#day-28---tuesday-november-3-2026---osaka) | 2 Hatobacho, Chuo-ku, Kobe 650-0042<br>Link: [hyogo-tourism.jp](https://www.hyogo-tourism.jp/world/spot/detail_1010.html) | A preserved 60m section of the Meriken Wharf quay wall, left cracked and tilted exactly as the Great Hanshin-Awaji Earthquake left it at 5:46am on 17 January 1995, a magnitude-7 quake that killed 4,751 people in Kobe alone. It occupies one corner of the otherwise cheerful Meriken Park waterfront. Free, flat, paved paths throughout. |
| Peace Memorial Park and the Atomic Bomb Dome exterior | [24](#day-24---friday-october-30-2026---kyoto-to-hiroshima) | 1-10 Otemachi, Naka-ku, Hiroshima 730-0051<br>Link: [peace-tourism.com](https://peace-tourism.com/en/spot/entry-194.html) | Peace Memorial Park is the riverside green space built after the war on what had been the city's busiest commercial district and the bombing's ground zero. This arrival-evening stop pairs a walk through the park with an exterior look at the Atomic Bomb Dome at its north edge. Free, outdoor, flat paths along the Motoyasu River, fully stroller-friendly. |

### Imperial and court history

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Imperial Palace East Gardens | [4](#day-4---saturday-october-10-2026---tokyo) | 1-1 Chiyoda, Chiyoda-ku, Tokyo 100-8111<br>Link: [kunaicho.go.jp](https://www.kunaicho.go.jp/e-event/higashigyoen02.html) | The public portion of the Imperial Palace grounds, the former Edo Castle's inner citadel, now a formal Japanese garden with the stone Tenshudai foundation, the burned keep's base, still standing. Free, with same-day entry via a QR or paper ticket at the gate and no advance booking. Flat gravel paths throughout except the Tenshudai's stone stairs, which need a carrier. |
| Kyoto Gyoen (parade viewing) | [16](#day-16---thursday-october-22-2026---kyoto) | Kyoto Gyoen (the park, not the walled Gosho), Kamigyo-ku, Kyoto 602-0881<br>Link: [policies.env.go.jp](https://policies.env.go.jp/national-garden/kyotogyoen/en/) | A large public park, about 1.3 sq km of open lawns, gravel paths and groves, free and unfenced, open at all hours. The walled and gated Kyoto Imperial Palace (Gosho) sits inside it but is a separate visit. On Jidai Matsuri day the park is the free viewing spot for the procession's opening stretch, with wide flat paths that give far more stroller room than the narrower downtown route. |
| Heian Jingu | [16](#day-16---thursday-october-22-2026---kyoto) | 97 Okazaki Nishitennocho, Sakyo-ku, Kyoto 606-8341<br>Link: [heianjingu.or.jp](https://www.heianjingu.or.jp/) | A shrine built in 1895 as a 5/8-scale replica of the original 8th-century Imperial Palace's main ceremonial hall, marking the 1100th anniversary of Kyoto's founding as capital. The shrine grounds and huge orange torii are free to enter. It is also where the Jidai Matsuri procession ends and where the festival itself originated. |
| Jidai Matsuri gagaku music | [16](#day-16---thursday-october-22-2026---kyoto) | Kyoto Gyoen (the park, not the walled Gosho), Kamigyo-ku, Kyoto 602-0881<br>Link: [heianjingu.or.jp](https://www.heianjingu.or.jp/ritual/jidaimatsuri/) | Two phoenix-crested mikoshi (portable shrines) near the end of the Jidai Matsuri procession are accompanied by gagaku, Japan's ancient court music, played on instruments largely unchanged since the Heian period. Free to hear from the roadside inside Kyoto Gyoen, no extra stop or ticket needed. It is heard alongside the day's free procession viewing, not sought out as a separate stop. |
| Kyoto Imperial Palace (Gosho) | [19](#day-19---sunday-october-25-2026---kyoto) | 3 Kyoto Gyoen, Kamigyo-ku, Kyoto 602-0881<br>Link: [kyoto-gosho.kunaicho.go.jp](https://kyoto-gosho.kunaicho.go.jp/en/visit) | The former residence of Japan's emperors from 1331 until the 1868 move to Tokyo, the walled and gated compound inside Kyoto Gyoen park. A 2016 policy change dropped the old reservation requirement, so entry is now free and walk-in, 9:00-16:20, closed Mondays. Visitors see the grounds and the exteriors of the Shishinden and Seiryuden from outside their fences; there is no access to building interiors. |

### Gardens

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ninomaru Garden, Imperial Palace East Gardens | [4](#day-4---saturday-october-10-2026---tokyo) | 1-1 Chiyoda, Chiyoda-ku, Tokyo 100-8111<br>Link: [kunaicho.go.jp](https://www.kunaicho.go.jp/e-event/higashigyoen02.html) | A formal Japanese garden within the free East Gardens, dating to the Edo period and restored in 1968. Flat gravel paths throughout, fully stroller-friendly. |
| Hamarikyu Gardens | [4](#day-4---saturday-october-10-2026---tokyo) | 1-1 Hamarikyu-teien, Chuo-ku, Tokyo 104-0046<br>Link: [tokyo-park.or.jp](https://www.tokyo-park.or.jp/teien/en/hama-rikyu/index.html) | A former Edo-period shogunal duck-hunting ground turned public garden, built around a tidal seawater pond fed directly from Tokyo Bay, unusual for a Japanese garden. A wooden bridge crosses to a floating teahouse serving matcha and wagashi. ¥300 per adult, wide gravel paths, mostly flat and stroller-friendly. |
| Meiji Jingu Inner Garden | [5](#day-5---sunday-october-11-2026---tokyo) | 1-1 Yoyogikamizonocho, Shibuya-ku, Tokyo 151-8557<br>Link: [meijijingu.or.jp](https://www.meijijingu.or.jp/en/whattosee/garden/) | An older Edo-period strolling garden within Meiji Jingu's grounds, with an iris pond, quieter and prettier than the free main shrine it sits behind. ¥500 per adult on top of the free shrine visit. Wide gravel paths, gentle grade, fully stroller-passable. |
| Shinjuku Gyoen | [10](#day-10---friday-october-16-2026---tokyo) | 11 Naitomachi, Shinjuku-ku, Tokyo 160-0014<br>Link: [policies.env.go.jp](https://policies.env.go.jp/national-garden/shinjukugyoen/index.html) | A large former imperial garden blending French formal, English landscape and traditional Japanese styles across 58 hectares in the middle of Shinjuku's skyscraper district. Wide lawns rather than a single walking route make it easy to just sit. ¥500 per adult, infants free, paved and gravel paths, fully stroller-friendly. |
| Sankeien Garden | [9](#day-9---thursday-october-15-2026---yokohama-day-trip) | 58-1 Honmokusannotani, Naka-ku, Yokohama, Kanagawa<br>Link: [sankeien.or.jp](https://www.sankeien.or.jp/) | A large traditional strolling garden built by a silk trader in 1906 around ponds and roughly 17 historic buildings relocated here from Kyoto, Kamakura and elsewhere, including a three-story pagoda visible above the treeline. ¥900 per adult. Main approach paths are stroller-passable; some inner bridges and paths have steps, so a carrier works better for the full loop. |
| Kubota Itchiku Art Museum garden | [12](#day-12---sunday-october-18-2026---kawaguchiko) | 2255 Kawaguchi, Fujikawaguchiko-machi, Minamitsuru-gun, Yamanashi 401-0304<br>Link: [itchiku-museum.com](https://www.itchiku-museum.com/) | A Gaudi-inspired stone garden beside the pyramid-shaped annex of the museum built around textile artist Itchiku Kubota's life work, giving somewhere to walk between the kimono-art rooms. Included in the ¥1,600 adult admission. Indoor and outdoor spaces are largely stroller-friendly. |
| Tenryu-ji, Sogenchi Garden | [17](#day-17---friday-october-23-2026---kyoto) | 68 Susukinobaba-cho, Saga Tenryuji, Ukyo-ku, Kyoto 616-8385<br>Link: [tenryuji.com](https://www.tenryuji.com/en/) | One of Japan's oldest surviving garden designs, built around a pond at this UNESCO World Heritage Zen temple and using the wooded Arashiyama hillside behind it as shakkei, borrowed scenery, so the mountain reads as part of the composition. Today's ¥500 ticket covers the garden only, not the temple hall interior. A flat gravel loop around the pond, stroller-workable though often busy. |
| Okochi Sanso Garden | [17](#day-17---friday-october-23-2026---kyoto) | 8 Sagaogurayama Tabuchiyamacho, Ukyo-ku, Kyoto 616-8394<br>Link: [ja.kyoto.travel](https://ja.kyoto.travel/tourism/single01.php?category_id=8&tourism_id=693) | A hillside villa garden built across the 1930s-50s by silent-film-era actor Okochi Denjiro, with sweeping views over Arashiyama's hills and, on a clear day, the Kyoto basin beyond. ¥1,000 per adult, including matcha and a seasonal sweet served on a viewing terrace at the end. Hillside gravel paths with some grade and a few steps, manageable with a carrier. |
| Heian Jingu Shin-en Garden | [16](#day-16---thursday-october-22-2026---kyoto) | 97 Okazaki Nishitennocho, Sakyo-ku, Kyoto 606-8341<br>Link: [heianjingu.or.jp](https://www.heianjingu.or.jp/) | A separate paid strolling pond garden behind the free Heian Jingu shrine grounds, added later than the shrine itself. ¥600 per adult. Gravel paths with a few stepping-stone crossings over the pond, a carrier is easier than a stroller at those crossings. |
| Ryoan-ji | [19](#day-19---sunday-october-25-2026---kyoto) | 13 Ryoanji Goryonoshitacho, Ukyo-ku, Kyoto 616-8001<br>Link: [ryoanji.jp](http://www.ryoanji.jp/) | Japan's most famous karesansui, or dry rock garden: a rectangle of raked white gravel with fifteen stones arranged in small groups on moss, no water and almost no plants, re-raked by the temple's monks each morning. It is laid out so at least one stone is always hidden from any single viewing point, an abstract composition meant for quiet looking rather than a landscape scene. ¥600 per adult, viewed from a raised wooden veranda, shoes off, so the stroller stays at the entrance and this is a carrier stop. |
| Isuien Garden | [20](#day-20---monday-october-26-2026---kyoto-day-trip-to-nara) | 74 Suimon-cho, Nara 630-8208<br>Link: [isuien.or.jp](https://isuien.or.jp/en/) | A strolling garden dating to the Edo and Meiji periods, split into two connected ponds, using shakkei (borrowed scenery) so that Todai-ji's Nandaimon Gate and the wooded hills beyond appear framed within the garden's own trees. ¥900 per adult. Gravel paths with some low stepping-stones, manageable with a stroller on the main loop but not flat throughout. |
| Shukkei-en | [26](#day-26---sunday-november-1-2026---hiroshima-city-day) | 2-11 Kaminobori-cho, Naka-ku, Hiroshima<br>Link: [shukkeien.jp](http://shukkeien.jp/) | A landscape garden laid out in 1620 around a central pond in the shukkei, "shrunken scenery," style, miniaturizing famous landscapes, mountains, valleys, a version of a Chinese lake, into one walkable circuit, a different style from Kyoto's dry rock gardens. It survived the bombing only as ruins and was rebuilt afterward. ¥260 per adult, main gravel loop stroller-workable, a few narrower bridges easier with a carrier. |
| Manyo Botanical Garden | [20](#day-20---monday-october-26-2026---kyoto-day-trip-to-nara) | 160 Kasuganocho, Nara 630-8212 (within Kasuga Taisha grounds)<br>Link: [kasugataisha.or.jp](http://kasugataisha.or.jp/manyou-s/) | A separate-ticket botanical garden inside Kasuga Taisha's grounds, planted with roughly 300 species named in Japan's oldest poetry anthology, the Man'yoshu, compiled around 759 CE. A quiet, uncrowded green space, notably a wisteria trellis, distinct from the shrine's formal architecture next door. ¥500 per adult, flat gravel and paved paths, stroller-manageable. |

### Onsen, sento and bathing

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Private kashikiri family bath | [13](#day-13---monday-october-19-2026---kawaguchiko) | Lakeland Hotel Mizunosato, 1070 Kodachi, Fujikawaguchiko, Minamitsuru-gun, Yamanashi 401-0302<br>Link: [mizunosato.jp](https://www.mizunosato.jp/en/) | A hot-spring bath rented by the room and time slot rather than shared with other guests, so the whole party, including both infants, bathes together in a single 40-minute booking. Communal onsen and sento baths commonly refuse visible tattoos, and their policies on admitting infants and diapers vary by facility and are often prohibitive, which is exactly what a private booking sidesteps. ¥2,750 flat per booking, not per adult, in slots between 15:00-20:00 and 05:00-09:00. |
| Local sento | [23](#day-23---thursday-october-29-2026---kyoto-low-key-day--packing) | Neighbourhood bathhouse near Omiya (address unverified) | A neighbourhood public bathhouse, built historically because most homes didn't have their own bath, heating tap or well water rather than drawing on a natural hot spring the way an onsen does. Bathing is nude and gender-separated, with washing and rinsing at stool-and-tap stations before the shared soaking tub. Most sento still refuse visible tattoos, and both the tattoo and infant/diaper policy vary by bathhouse and are often prohibitive, so confirm ahead rather than assume; ¥490 per adult. |

### Traditional lodging

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lakeland Hotel Mizunosato (ryokan) | [13](#day-13---monday-october-19-2026---kawaguchiko) | 1070 Kodachi, Fujikawaguchiko, Minamitsuru-gun, Yamanashi 401-0302<br>Link: [mizunosato.jp](https://www.mizunosato.jp/en/) | A lakeside ryokan on Lake Kawaguchi, and the trip's one night on tatami and futon rather than a Western-style bed. The room-only rate is ¥41,580 for 3 adults (¥13,860 per adult, room-only), with kaiseki dinner and Japanese breakfast booked as separate half-board add-ons and a rentable private kashikiri bath at ¥2,750 per 40 minutes, in slots between 15:00-20:00 and 05:00-09:00. The exact five-person room configuration and any infant futon or meal surcharge are not published on the ryokan's own site, so confirm both by phone before booking. |

### Markets and street stalls

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Nakamise-dori | [2](#day-2---thursday-october-8-2026---tokyo) | 2-3-1 Asakusa, Taito-ku, Tokyo 111-0032<br>Link: [senso-ji.jp](https://www.senso-ji.jp/) | The covered approach street to Senso-ji, lined with snack and souvenir stalls leading to the Kaminarimon lantern gate. Free to browse, grounds always open. It's genuinely too dense for a stroller by mid-morning, so use the carrier here. |
| Ameyoko | [3](#day-3---friday-october-9-2026---tokyo) | Ameyoko market, Taito-ku, Tokyo (address unverified)<br>Link: [ameyoko.net](https://www.ameyoko.net/) | A street market running between Ueno and Okachimachi stations, born out of a postwar black market and now mixing cut-price clothing and cosmetics with seafood stalls and street food. This visit is a food stop - takoyaki, pan-fried gyoza, yakitori skewers eaten standing. Free to browse. |
| Yanaka Ginza | [6](#day-6---monday-october-12-2026---tokyo-sports-day) | Yanaka, Taito-ku (nearest station Sendagi or Nippori; address unverified)<br>Link: [yanaka-ginza.com](https://yanaka-ginza.com/) | An old-fashioned shotengai of roughly 60 small shops - senbei crackers, menchi-katsu stalls, cat-themed goods - that survived Tokyo's postwar rebuilding largely intact. The best "old Tokyo streetscape" browsing outside Asakusa, and much less crowded. Free to browse, flat and stroller-friendly outside peak weekend hours. |
| Toyosu Market (outer area) | [8](#day-8---wednesday-october-14-2026---tokyo-odaiba--toyosu) | Toyosu Market outer area, Koto-ku (nearest station Shijo-mae; address unverified)<br>Link: [shijou.metro.tokyo.lg.jp](https://www.shijou.metro.tokyo.lg.jp/) | The outer, publicly accessible ring of Tokyo's wholesale fish and produce market, grazed here for breakfast - a grilled scallop skewer, tamagoyaki, a few pieces of nigiri, hojicha tea - stall by stall rather than at a sit-down sushi counter. Entry is free; public hours run roughly 5am-5pm and the market closes on scattered days, so check before going. |
| Kobo-ichi, To-ji | [15](#day-15---wednesday-october-21-2026---kyoto) | To-ji, 1 Kujocho, Minami-ku, Kyoto 601-8473<br>Link: [toji.or.jp](https://toji.or.jp/en/) | A monthly temple market: a flea-and-food market tied to a temple's own history rather than staged for tourists. Kobo-ichi falls on the 21st of every month, marking the death anniversary of Kobo Daishi, and fills To-ji's grounds with roughly 1,000 stalls of antiques, vintage kimono, ceramics, plants and genuine street food. Free to enter, published hours roughly 8:30-17:30; open gravel approach paths take a stroller, but the packed stall rows call for a carrier. |
| Nishiki Market | [15](#day-15---wednesday-october-21-2026---kyoto) | Nishikikoji-dori, Nakagyo-ku, Kyoto 604-8054<br>Link: [kyoto-nishiki.or.jp](https://www.kyoto-nishiki.or.jp/en/) | A roughly 400m covered arcade of about 130 shops trading since the Edo period as "Kyoto's Kitchen" - fresh seafood, pickles, knives, tea and skewered snacks. Free to walk, and the best way to taste a range of Kyoto specialties without a sit-down meal, though several stalls now post signs asking visitors not to eat while walking. The covered floor is stroller-workable but narrow and can be shoulder-to-shoulder at midday. |
| Tenjin-ichi, Kitano Tenmangu | [19](#day-19---sunday-october-25-2026---kyoto) | Kitano Tenmangu, Bakuro-cho, Kamigyo-ku, Kyoto 602-8386<br>Link: [kitanotenmangu.or.jp](https://kitanotenmangu.or.jp/) | Kitano Tenmangu's own monthly temple market, the same kind of centuries-old, fixed-date flea-and-food market as To-ji's Kobo-ichi, held on the 25th of every month to mark the death anniversary of the god of learning. Up to around 1,000 stalls fill the shrine grounds and surrounding streets with antiques, plants, kimono and street snacks. Free to enter, published hours roughly 6:00 until the stalls wind down; the shrine's open paths take a stroller, but the packed aisles are carrier-only. |
| Nankinmachi (Kobe Chinatown) | [28](#day-28---tuesday-november-3-2026---osaka) | Nankinmachi, Sakaemachi-dori, Chuo-ku, Kobe 650-0023<br>Link: [nankinmachi.or.jp](https://nankinmachi.or.jp/) | One of Japan's three historic Chinatowns, a compact grid of streets given over to pork buns, ebi mayo buns and char siu skewers eaten standing. Free to walk, a short stroll inland from Meriken Park. This is a market now aimed squarely at visitors rather than a residential Chinatown's own daily shopping. |
| Kuromon Ichiba Market | [29](#day-29---wednesday-november-4-2026---osaka) | Kuromon Ichiba Market, 2-4-1 Nipponbashi, Chuo-ku, Osaka 542-0073<br>Link: [kuromon.com](https://kuromon.com/en/) | A roughly 580m covered market of about 170 stalls, historically a wholesale fish and produce market feeding local restaurants. Most stalls today sell grill-it-and-eat-it single portions - nigiri, grilled wagyu skewers, tamagoyaki, oysters on the half shell - priced for visitors rather than a neighborhood greengrocer, which is the honest reason costs run higher than they look. Free to enter and browse. |

The outdoor life-size Unicorn Gundam statue that used to stand outside ended its display 31 Aug 2026. Free to browse, indoor, flat, stroller-fine. |

Entry is by advance reservation only, released on a fixed monthly date with no walk-up sales, and no strollers are permitted indoors. |

Free and open 24 hours. The shrine and its surrounding Fushimi neighbourhood are the real-world setting of Inari, Konkon, Koi Iroha. The paved loop to Yotsutsuji is stroller-workable; the mountain beyond turns to stone steps, carrier-only. |
| Uji riverbank | [21](#day-21---tuesday-october-27-2026---kyoto-day-trip-to-uji) | Uji River, Uji-shi, Kyoto (riverside path, no single address)<br>Link: [kyoto-uji-kankou.or.jp](https://www.kyoto-uji-kankou.or.jp/index-en.html) | A free riverside walk along the Uji River, whose bridge and the nearby Keihan Uji Station all appear as real, filmable locations in Kyoto Animation's Sound! Euphonium. No ticket and no separate stop - it folds into the day's midday rest. |
| Pokemon Center Osaka DX | [27](#day-27---monday-november-2-2026---hiroshima-to-osaka) | Daimaru Shinsaibashi Main Bldg, 9F, 1-7-1 Shinsaibashisuji, Chuo-ku, Osaka 542-8501<br>Link: [shop.pokemon.co.jp](https://shop.pokemon.co.jp/en/shop/pokemoncenter-osakadx/) | An official Pokemon retail store selling plushes, trading cards, region-exclusive merchandise and stationery, with a life-size photo-op Pikachu near the entrance. Free to enter, no ticket needed. Unlike some other Pokemon Centers, this one has no attached cafe - Pokemon Cafe Osaka is a separate premises elsewhere in the city. Elevator-served department-store floor. |
| Den Den Town | [29](#day-29---wednesday-november-4-2026---osaka) | Nipponbashi/Ota Road, Naniwa-ku/Chuo-ku, Osaka (district, no single address)<br>Link: [osaka-info.jp](https://osaka-info.jp/en/spot/nippombashi-denden-town/) | Osaka's own anime and hobby-shop district, a roughly 1km strip with over 150 shops - Animate, Yellow Submarine, Super Potato, K-Books - selling figures, Gunpla, cards and doujinshi. It shares some chains with Tokyo's Akihabara but reads smaller, less crowded and more retro-collector in feel. Free to browse; flat, wide shopping street, stroller-workable outside peak hours. |

### Technology and science

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| National Museum of Nature and Science (Kahaku) | [3](#day-3---friday-october-9-2026---tokyo) | 7-20 Ueno Park, Taito-ku, Tokyo 110-8718<br>Link: [kahaku.go.jp](https://www.kahaku.go.jp/english/) | Japan's national museum of natural history and science: a full dinosaur skeleton hall, the actual Lambda 4S rocket that launched Japan's first satellite in 1970, space suits, and deep-sea exploration displays. ¥620/adult. Indoor and elevator-served throughout, open until 20:00 on Fridays. |
| teamLab Planets TOKYO DMM | [8](#day-8---wednesday-october-14-2026---tokyo-odaiba--toyosu) | Toyosu 6-1-16, Koto-ku, Tokyo 135-0061<br>Link: [teamlab.art](https://www.teamlab.art/e/planets/) | A barefoot, walk-through digital art museum: visitors wade ankle-deep through a mirrored water room and move through immersive projection-mapped spaces. ¥5,300/adult, timed entry that sells out ahead of the date. Guests get wet to the knee, so pack a full change of clothes; strollers are left at entrance lockers, and under-3s need a registered zero-cost ticket rather than riding along in a carrier. |
| Cup Noodles Museum | [9](#day-9---thursday-october-15-2026---yokohama-day-trip) | 2-3-4 Shinko, Naka-ku, Yokohama, Kanagawa 231-0001<br>Link: [cupnoodles-museum.jp](https://www.cupnoodles-museum.jp/en/yokohama/) | A museum on instant ramen's invention by Momofuku Ando, with a giant noodle-themed exhibit hall. ¥1,000/adult including the My CUPNOODLES Factory workshop; the exhibit hall alone is ¥500. Indoor, elevators, stroller-friendly; workshop tickets are sold same-day on-site and can sell out on busy days. |
| Gundam Base Tokyo | [8](#day-8---wednesday-october-14-2026---tokyo-odaiba--toyosu) | DiverCity Tokyo Plaza, 7F, 1-1-10 Aomi, Koto-ku, Tokyo 135-0064<br>Link: [gundam-base.net](https://www.gundam-base.net/) | A free Gunpla retail floor rather than a museum: full-wall model displays and build-and-buy kits floor to ceiling. Indoor, flat, stroller-fine; small Gunpla kits run roughly ¥1,500-4,000 if anyone wants a souvenir. |

### Art and museums

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tokyo National Museum | [3](#day-3---friday-october-9-2026---tokyo) | 13-9 Uenokoen, Taito-ku, Tokyo 110-8712<br>Link: [tnm.jp](https://www.tnm.jp/?lang=en) | Japan's oldest and largest museum, covering samurai armor, swords, ceramics and Buddhist sculpture across several permanent galleries. Booked as permanent-collection-only this visit, skipping the rotating special exhibition. ¥1,000/adult, elevators throughout. |
| Kubota Itchiku Art Museum | [12](#day-12---sunday-october-18-2026---kawaguchiko) | 2255 Kawaguchi, Fujikawaguchiko-machi, Minamitsuru-gun, Yamanashi 401-0304<br>Link: [itchiku-museum.com](https://www.itchiku-museum.com/) | A museum devoted to textile artist Itchiku Kubota, showing kimono art depicting Mt. Fuji made using his revived tsujigahana dyeing technique. ¥1,600/adult, including a Gaudi-inspired stone garden beside the kimono-art annex. |
| Itsukushima Treasure Hall | [25](#day-25---saturday-october-31-2026---miyajima-itsukushima) | Adjacent to Itsukushima Shrine, 1-1 Miyajima-cho, Hatsukaichi 739-0588<br>Link: [itsukushimajinja.jp](https://www.itsukushimajinja.jp/en/) | A small museum attached to the shrine holding centuries of donated ceremonial treasures - swords, masks, sutras, court robes - given by samurai and nobility over the shrine's long history. ¥300/adult. Indoor and flat, though narrow display aisles mean a stroller is often left parked at the door. |
| Tale of Genji Museum | [21](#day-21---tuesday-october-27-2026---kyoto-day-trip-to-uji) | 45-26 Higashiuchi, Uji-shi, Kyoto 611-0021<br>Link: [city.uji.kyoto.jp](https://www.city.uji.kyoto.jp/site/genji/) | A small, air-conditioned museum devoted to the Uji Chapters, the final ten chapters of The Tale of Genji, with a scale palace model and a short film. ¥600/adult. Fully flat, stroller-accessible, and rarely crowded. |
| Teradaya Inn | [22](#day-22---wednesday-october-28-2026---kyoto-day-trip-to-the-fushimi-sake-district) | 263 Minamihama-cho, Fushimi-ku, Kyoto<br>Link: [kyoto.travel](https://kyoto.travel/en/destinations/teradaya-inn/) | A reconstructed Edo-era inn turned house-museum, site of an 1866 attack on the samurai Sakamoto Ryoma, one of the key figures behind the Meiji Restoration. ¥600/adult. The interior is a traditional wooden inn with a raised tatami floor and a step up at the entrance, so plan to carry rather than wheel the stroller inside. |
| Peace Memorial Museum | [26](#day-26---sunday-november-1-2026---hiroshima-city-day) | 1-2 Nakajima-cho, Naka-ku, Hiroshima<br>Link: [hpmmuseum.jp](https://hpmmuseum.jp/?lang=eng) | The city's central museum on the atomic bombing, built around personal artifacts, photographs and survivor testimony; the lower-floor galleries are graphic and confronting. ¥200/adult, free under 18. The party splits here, with one or two adults going through while the others stay outside with the children. |
| National Museum of Art, Osaka | [28](#day-28---tuesday-november-3-2026---osaka) | 4-2-55 Nakanoshima, Kita-ku, Osaka 530-0005<br>Link: [nmao.go.jp](https://www.nmao.go.jp/en/) | A contemporary and modern art museum whose entire exhibition space sits underground - the only thing visible at street level is a cluster of curved steel pylons by architect Cesar Pelli marking the entrance. About ¥430/adult, free for everyone on 3 Nov (Bunka no Hi / Culture Day). Fully underground and lift-served, no stairs-only stretches; closed Mondays. |

### Crafts and hands-on activities

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Plastic food-sample workshop | [6](#day-6---monday-october-12-2026---tokyo-sports-day) | Ganso Shokuhin Sample-ya, Kappabashi, 3-7-6 Nishiasakusa, Taito-ku, Tokyo<br>Link: [ganso-sample.com](https://www.ganso-sample.com/experience/kappa_menu1/) | A hands-on workshop where you hand-shape and colour a piece of "food" (tempura and lettuce) using the traditional hot-wax method under a craftsperson's guidance, on Tokyo's wholesale restaurant-supply street. Reservation required; instruction is typically Japanese-only, so confirm English support when booking. The hot wax itself is not for toddler hands, so this is realistically one or two adults working while a third minds the infants. |
| My CUPNOODLES Factory workshop | [9](#day-9---thursday-october-15-2026---yokohama-day-trip) | Cup Noodles Museum, 2-3-4 Shinko, Naka-ku, Yokohama, Kanagawa 231-0001<br>Link: [cupnoodles-museum.jp](https://www.cupnoodles-museum.jp/en/yokohama/attractions/mc-factory/) | Design and seal your own cup of instant noodles to take home, a hands-on add-on to the base museum visit. ¥500 per cup on top of the ¥500 base admission. Tickets are sold same-day on-site and can sell out on busy days. |
| Taihoan tea ceremony | [21](#day-21---tuesday-october-27-2026---kyoto-day-trip-to-uji) | 1-5 Togawa, Uji-shi, Kyoto<br>Link: [kyoto-uji-kankou.or.jp](https://www.kyoto-uji-kankou.or.jp/taihoan-en.html) | Whisk your own bowl of matcha under a host's guidance, paired with a seasonal wagashi sweet, at Uji's municipal tea house across from Byodo-in's Phoenix Hall. ¥1,000/adult. Seating is low tatami-style with no chair option; toddlers are welcome alongside a parent. |
| Kimono rental, worn for the day | [23](#day-23---thursday-october-29-2026---kyoto-low-key-day--packing) | Yumeyakata Gojo Shop, 353 Shiogamacho, Shimogyo-ku, Kyoto 600-8103<br>Link: [en-kyoto.yumeyakata.com](https://www.en-kyoto.yumeyakata.com/) | A full kimono set - garment, obi, tabi socks, and a bag - fitted by staff in about 20-30 minutes and worn out for the day, returned by 17:30 the same evening. ¥5,280/adult, ¥15,840 for three. Wooden geta sandals plus a stroller are a genuinely awkward combination on anything but flat pavement. |
| Kimono rental, full day | [23](#day-23---thursday-october-29-2026---kyoto-low-key-day--packing) | Yumeyakata Gojo Shop, Shimogyo-ku, Kyoto<br>Link: [en-kyoto.yumeyakata.com](https://www.en-kyoto.yumeyakata.com/) | The same rental worn all day rather than for a single outing. ¥5,280/adult. A wrapped kimono skirt and a snugly tied obi are hard to nurse or change a diaper in, so this is booked as an adults-only outfit. |
| Nishijin hand-loom weaving | [23](#day-23---thursday-october-29-2026---kyoto-low-key-day--packing) | Horikawa-dori Imadegawa-minami-iri Nishigawa, Kamigyo-ku, Kyoto 602-8216<br>Link: [nishijin.or.jp](https://nishijin.or.jp/eng/nishijin_textile_center1/) | Sit at a mini hand-loom and weave a small cloth, about 20x30cm, to take home, guided step by step in Kyoto's roughly 1,200-year-old Nishijin brocade-weaving tradition. ¥2,530/adult; reservation required at least 2 days ahead via the official site. A mini loom needs both hands and a foot treadle, so adults weave one at a time while the others mind the infants. |
| Saiko Iyashi-no-Sato Nenba | [13](#day-13---monday-october-19-2026---kawaguchiko) | 2710 Nenba, Saiko, Fujikawaguchiko-machi, Minamitsuru-gun, Yamanashi 401-0332<br>Link: [saikoiyashinosatonenba.jp](https://saikoiyashinosatonenba.jp/en/) | A reconstructed thatched-roof farming village on the shore of Lake Saiko, rebuilt after a 1966 landslide destroyed the original hamlet; about 20 restored buildings house small craft workshops, food stalls and a folk museum to browse rather than a single formal activity. ¥500/adult. Flat, unpaved-but-firm paths, stroller-manageable with care at the thatched-building thresholds. |
| Gekkeikan Okura Sake Museum tasting | [22](#day-22---wednesday-october-28-2026---kyoto-day-trip-to-the-fushimi-sake-district) | 247 Minamihama-cho, Fushimi-ku, Kyoto 612-8660<br>Link: [gekkeikan.com](https://www.gekkeikan.com/museum/fushimi/) | A museum inside a working sake brewery, set in a 1909 storehouse, on the process by which steamed rice is converted to fermentable sugar by koji mold and then fermented into sake. Admission includes three tastings and a souvenir bottle to take home. ¥600/adult, free for ages 12 and under. The building is flat and stroller-accessible; the tasting counter itself is standing-room. |

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Yoyogi Park | [5](#day-5---sunday-october-11-2026---tokyo) | Yoyogikamizonocho, Shibuya-ku, Tokyo (nearest station Harajuku; address unverified)<br>Link: [tokyo-park.or.jp](https://www.tokyo-park.or.jp/park/yoyogi/) | Tokyo's largest and most famous city park, wide open lawns beside Meiji Jingu's forest. Free, flat paved and grass paths throughout, fully stroller-friendly. This is where the day's midday rest block, packed lunch and the Sunday rockabilly dancers all happen. |
| Odaiba Marine Park | [8](#day-8---wednesday-october-14-2026---tokyo-odaiba--toyosu) | 1-4 Daiba, Minato-ku, Tokyo 135-0091<br>Link: [gotokyo.org](https://www.gotokyo.org/en/spot/39/index.html) | A flat waterfront park and small artificial beach on Tokyo Bay, facing the Rainbow Bridge and a half-scale replica Statue of Liberty. Free, wide paved promenade throughout, no stairs, a quiet early-morning start before the crowds build. |
| Chureito Pagoda / Arakurayama Sengen Park | [11](#day-11---saturday-october-17-2026---tokyo--kawaguchiko) | 3353-1 Arakura, Fujiyoshida, Yamanashi 403-0011<br>Link: [city.fujiyoshida.yamanashi.jp](https://www.city.fujiyoshida.yamanashi.jp/page/13101.html) | A five-story vermilion pagoda on a hillside above Lake Kawaguchi, framed with Mt. Fuji behind it - the single most photographed view in the Fuji Five Lakes region. The viewing platform is reached by 398 stone steps or a longer slope path, both carrier-only; one adult carries one infant up while the other two adults and the stroller wait at the flat lower shrine grounds. Free entry, no booking needed. |
| Oishi Park | [12](#day-12---sunday-october-18-2026---kawaguchiko) | 2585 Oishi, Fujikawaguchiko-machi, Minamitsuru-gun, Yamanashi 401-0305<br>Link: [fkchannel.jp](https://www.fkchannel.jp/naturelivingcenter/oishipark/) | A free lakeside park and flower garden on Lake Kawaguchi's north shore, laid out to frame Mt. Fuji across the water without any stair climb. Go in the morning, the reliable window for a clear mountain before afternoon cloud builds. Flat gravel and grass paths, fully stroller-friendly, with a small cafe and restrooms on site. |
| Lake Kawaguchi sightseeing cruise | [12](#day-12---sunday-october-18-2026---kawaguchiko) | Funatsuhama Pier, Kawaguchiko, ~10 min walk from Kawaguchiko Stn (street address unverified)<br>Link: [fujikyu.co.jp](https://www.fujikyu.co.jp/en/leisure/leisure15.html) | A 20-minute loop around Lake Kawaguchi passing under the Kawaguchiko Ohashi bridge and around Unoshima Island, a water-level angle on Fuji different from the park or ropeway view. ¥1,200/adult, infants free. Boarding is via a short gangway with a handrail, not wheel-on, but an adult can carry a folded stroller aboard. |
| Kawaguchiko Tenjozan Ropeway | [12](#day-12---sunday-october-18-2026---kawaguchiko) | 1163 Azagawa, Fujikawaguchiko-machi, Minamitsuru-gun, Yamanashi 401-0303<br>Link: [mtfujiropeway.jp](https://www.mtfujiropeway.jp/en/) | A cable car climbing about 400m up Mt. Tenjo in under three minutes to a summit deck with a panorama of Mt. Fuji across the full width of Lake Kawaguchi. ¥1,000/adult, infants free. Both stations are step-free and the cabin takes a folded stroller or a carrier-worn infant; morning is the more reliable window for the mountain. |
| Oshino Hakkai | [13](#day-13---monday-october-19-2026---kawaguchiko) | Oshino, Minamitsuru-gun, Yamanashi 401-0511<br>Link: [oshino-navi.com](https://oshino-navi.com/en/) | A cluster of eight spring-fed ponds filled with Mt. Fuji's own filtered snowmelt, the free public path between them being the actual draw. One of the eight, Nigori-ike, sits inside a separate paid garden with its own admission (+¥300). Flat, paved, stroller-friendly throughout, and busiest by late morning. |
| Yuigahama Beach | [7](#day-7---tuesday-october-13-2026---kamakura-day-trip) | Yuigahama, Kamakura, Kanagawa (nearest station Yuigahama, Enoden; address unverified)<br>Link: [visit.trip-kamakura.com](https://visit.trip-kamakura.com/things-to-do/yuigahama-beach/) | Kamakura's town beach, a wide stretch of gray-brown sand facing Sagami Bay. Free, an easy wind-down after two temples, flat sand approach from the road, stroller-passable to the sand's edge; carrier or being carried is easier once actually on the sand. |
| Arashiyama Bamboo Grove | [17](#day-17---friday-october-23-2026---kyoto) | Sagaogurayama Bamboo Grove path, Ukyo-ku, Kyoto, near Arashiyama Stn (Hankyu) / JR Saga-Arashiyama (address unverified)<br>Link: [kyoto.travel](https://kyoto.travel/en/areas/saga-arashiyama/) | A several-hundred-metre path cut through a dense grove of towering bamboo, one of the most photographed natural scenes in Japan. Free, always open, no ticket, best in the first hour after sunrise before tour groups arrive. The path is flat packed gravel and stroller-fine, with no shade or seating along it. |
| Togetsukyo Bridge and riverside | [17](#day-17---friday-october-23-2026---kyoto) | Ukyo-ku, Kyoto, over the Katsura/Hozu River, near Arashiyama Stn (Hankyu) (address unverified)<br>Link: [kyoto.travel](https://kyoto.travel/en/destinations/togetsukyo-bridge/) | "Moon Crossing Bridge," Arashiyama's postcard landmark, the current span dating to 1934. The wide river and forested hillside behind it are the real draw. Free to walk, flat pavement, open riverside space for a toddler to stretch their legs off the stroller. |
| Philosopher's Path | [16](#day-16---thursday-october-22-2026---kyoto) | Tetsugaku-no-michi, Sakyo-ku, Kyoto, Nanzenji to Eikan-do stretch, near Keage Stn (Tozai Line) (address unverified)<br>Link: [kyoto.travel](https://kyoto.travel/en/destinations/philosophers-path-tetsugakunomichi/) | A stone path along a canal between Nanzenji and Ginkaku-ji, named for 20th-century Kyoto University philosopher Nishida Kitaro, who reportedly walked it daily for meditation. Free, flat, and quiet compared to the temple crowds; late-October color is only just starting to turn at the canal's edge. |
| Yanaka Cemetery | [6](#day-6---monday-october-12-2026---tokyo-sports-day) | Yanaka, Taito-ku, Tokyo (nearest station Nippori; address unverified)<br>Link: [tokyo-park.or.jp](https://www.tokyo-park.or.jp/reien/yanaka/) | A large, tree-lined Edo-era cemetery of over 7,000 graves laid out in quiet gridded lanes lined with cherry trees, more a peaceful historic walk than a somber one. Free; paths are paved but uneven in places, so a carrier is the better call than the stroller. |
| Nara Park (deer) | [20](#day-20---monday-october-26-2026---kyoto-day-trip-to-nara) | Nara Park (district; address unverified)<br>Link: [pref.nara.lg.jp](https://www.pref.nara.lg.jp/site/park/index.html) | Home to roughly 1,200 wild sika deer that have wandered the park freely for over a thousand years, historically protected as messengers of the local Shinto gods. Free to watch; ¥200 per pack of rice crackers to feed them by hand. The deer are pushy once food is out, so keep the pack out of a toddler's reach and hold crackers at adult height. |
| Momijidani Park | [25](#day-25---saturday-october-31-2026---miyajima-itsukushima) | Miyajima-cho, Hatsukaichi-shi, Hiroshima, at the Miyajima Ropeway base station (address unverified)<br>Link: [miyajima.or.jp](https://www.miyajima.or.jp/english/spot/spot_momiji.html) | A free, shaded maple valley park at the base of the Miyajima Ropeway, benches throughout. It doubles as the day's midday rest block: one adult naps with the stroller and one infant here while the other two adults ride the ropeway. |
| Meriken Park | [28](#day-28---tuesday-november-3-2026---osaka) | 2 Hatobacho, Chuo-ku, Kobe 650-0042<br>Link: [feel-kobe.jp](https://www.feel-kobe.jp/en/attractions/detail_1006.html) | A flat harbourside park facing Kobe Port Tower, with the "Be Kobe" sign as the current photo spot. One corner holds the Port of Kobe Earthquake Memorial Park, a 60m section of quay wall left cracked by the 1995 Great Hanshin-Awaji Earthquake. Free, fully stroller-friendly, Nankinmachi a short walk inland. |

Fuji snowmelt. The free public path between the ponds is the draw; flat, paved, stroller-friendly throughout, busiest by late morning. |

Flat, unpaved-but-firm paths, stroller-manageable with some care at the thatched-building thresholds. |

Free to browse; the shopping street is flat and stroller-friendly outside peak hours, the cemetery's paths are paved but uneven, better with a carrier. |

Entirely flat, paved and stroller-friendly, with benches along the water and none of central Kyoto's crowds. |

### Streets, lanes and canal walks

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Neighbourhood walk | [14](#day-14---tuesday-october-20-2026---kawaguchiko--kyoto) | Shijo-Omiya, Kyoto, near Omiya Stn, Hankyu Kyoto Line (address unverified) | A free, unplanned walk around the hotel's neighbourhood to get oriented before the ten-night Kyoto stay begins - no set route or paid stop, just enough to find the nearest konbini and bus stop. Flat city streets, fully stroller-friendly, deliberately light after a transfer-heavy morning. |
| Gion district walk | [18](#day-18---saturday-october-24-2026---kyoto) | Hanamikoji-dori, Gion, Higashiyama-ku, Kyoto, near Gion-Shijo Stn (Keihan) (address unverified)<br>Link: [kyoto.travel](https://kyoto.travel/en/areas/gion-kiyomizu/) | Kyoto's most famous geiko and maiko entertainment district, a working quarter since the Edo period, lined with narrow-fronted wooden machiya townhouses. Several private side-lanes ban tourist photography, with fines reported up to ¥10,000 for photographing geiko or maiko without consent. Flat and stroller-fine; the real geiko/maiko traffic happens after dark, past infant bedtime. |
| Shirakawa Canal | [18](#day-18---saturday-october-24-2026---kyoto) | Shirakawa Canal, Gion, Higashiyama-ku, Kyoto, near Gion-Shijo Stn (Keihan) (address unverified) | A narrow, willow-lined canal past traditional teahouses, one of Gion's most photographed corners and quieter than Hanamikoji-dori itself. Free, flat stone walkway alongside the water, stroller-fine. |
| Pontocho evening lantern walk | [18](#day-18---saturday-october-24-2026---kyoto) | Pontocho, Nakagyo-ku, Kyoto, between Sanjo-dori and Shijo-dori, one block west of the Kamogawa (address unverified)<br>Link: [japan.travel](https://www.japan.travel/en/spot/79/) | One of Kyoto's five historic geiko districts: a single pedestrian lane about 500m long and only 2-3m wide, lined with machiya, hanging lanterns and around 80 restaurants. Free to walk through after dark; the priciest restaurants refuse first-time or foreign walk-ins (ichigen-san okotowari), so the plan walks through only. The riverside kawayuka dining platforms are dismantled by the end of September, so this October visit sees the plain alley rather than the summer platforms. Narrow and crowded in the evening, carrier over stroller. |
| Kamo riverbank walk (in kimono) | [23](#day-23---thursday-october-29-2026---kyoto-low-key-day--packing) | Kamo River near Gojo, Shimogyo-ku, Kyoto (district; address unverified)<br>Link: [japan.travel](https://www.japan.travel/en/spot/1183/) | The Kamo River's grassy, paved banks near the kimono shop, today's photo backdrop while the three adults are dressed for the day. Free, and the plan stays on the paved path throughout since geta sandals are slower and stiffer than sneakers on anything uneven; stroller-friendly for whichever infant isn't being carried. |
| Dotonbori canal walk | [27](#day-27---monday-november-2-2026---hiroshima-to-osaka), [29](#day-29---wednesday-november-4-2026---osaka) | Dotonbori, Chuo-ku, Osaka (district; nearest station Namba, address unverified)<br>Link: [osaka-info.jp](https://osaka-info.jp/en/spot/dotonbori/) | Osaka's best-known nightlife strip, a canal-side promenade lined with oversized illuminated signs, the animated Glico "running man" billboard the classic photo spot. Free, flat and stroller-workable along the main promenade, though crowded and slow-moving after dark. |
| Canal-side brewery walk, Chushojima | [22](#day-22---wednesday-october-28-2026---kyoto-day-trip-to-the-fushimi-sake-district) | Canal streets around the Gekkeikan Okura Sake Museum, Chushojima, Fushimi-ku, Kyoto (district; address unverified)<br>Link: [kyoto.travel](https://kyoto.travel/en/see-and-do/fushimi.html) | A free stroll along the old Fushimi river port's canal, past rows of white-plastered sake brewery warehouses with black wooden trim, some dating to the Edo period when this was the transshipment point for sake and rice moving between Kyoto and Osaka by boat. Entirely flat, paved and stroller-friendly, with benches along the water. |
| Higashiyama lanes, Sannenzaka and Ninenzaka | [18](#day-18---saturday-october-24-2026---kyoto) | Sannenzaka and Ninenzaka slopes, Higashiyama-ku, Kyoto, near the Kiyomizu-michi bus stop (address unverified)<br>Link: [ja.kyoto.travel](https://ja.kyoto.travel/komafuda/show.php?id=2239&lang=en) | Two connected stone-paved lanes climbing to Kiyomizu-dera, lined with preserved wooden shopfronts and teahouses - the closest thing to a preserved Edo-period streetscape in central Kyoto. Free to walk. The paving is old, uneven flagstone on a slope, stepped and steep in places, so carrier rather than stroller. |

### Festivals and seasonal events

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Jidai Matsuri | [16](#day-16---thursday-october-22-2026---kyoto) | Kyoto Gyoen (the park, not the walled Gosho), Kamigyo-ku, Kyoto 602-0881<br>Link: [heianjingu.or.jp](https://www.heianjingu.or.jp/ritual/jidaimatsuri/) | Kyoto's "Festival of the Ages," held every 22 October since 1895 - in 2026 that is a Thursday, the date this visit falls on. About 2,000 participants march in accurate historical costume spanning Kyoto's 1,000+ years as capital, processing from the Imperial Palace area to Heian Jingu roughly 12:00 to 14:30. Watched free from inside Kyoto Gyoen's wide gravel paths, no grandstand seat bought (paid seats run about ¥5,000-15,500). Two phoenix-crested portable shrines near the procession's end bring gagaku court music, audible free from the roadside. |
| Tokyo Rockabilly Club dancers | [5](#day-5---sunday-october-11-2026---tokyo) | Yoyogi Park, Harajuku entrance plaza, Shibuya-ku, Tokyo (exact address unverified) | A free, unticketed open-air dance gathering going back to the 1980s: crews in full 1950s greaser style, leather jackets and pompadours, dance to rock and roll off a portable sound system most Sundays. Welcoming to onlookers and viewable from the sidelines or a stroller. Weather-dependent and not guaranteed, commonly starting early-to-mid afternoon and running an hour or more. |
| Kobo-ichi market | [15](#day-15---wednesday-october-21-2026---kyoto) | To-ji, 1 Kujocho, Minami-ku, Kyoto 601-8473<br>Link: [toji.or.jp](https://toji.or.jp/mieku/) | A monthly temple market on the 21st of every month, marking Kobo Daishi's death anniversary, running unbroken for generations rather than staged for tourists. Roughly 1,000 stalls fill To-ji's grounds beneath its five-story pagoda with antiques, vintage kimono, ceramics, plants, and street food. Free entry, roughly 8:30-17:30; the wide approach is stroller-workable but the packed stall aisles favor a carrier. |
| Tenjin-ichi market | [19](#day-19---sunday-october-25-2026---kyoto) | Kitano Tenmangu, Bakuro-cho, Kamigyo-ku, Kyoto 602-8386<br>Link: [kitanotenmangu.or.jp](https://kitanotenmangu.or.jp/en/) | Kitano Tenmangu's own monthly market, held on the 25th to mark the death anniversary of Sugawara no Michizane, god of learning. Up to around 1,000 stalls fill the shrine grounds and surrounding streets with antiques, plants, kimono, and street snacks. Free, roughly 6:00 until stalls wind down in the afternoon; the shrine's open paths take a stroller, the packed aisles want a carrier. |
| Midosuji "Hikari no Kyoen" illumination | [28](#day-28---tuesday-november-3-2026---osaka) | Midosuji Avenue, between the Hanshin-mae and Namba-nishiguchi intersections, Chuo-ku, Osaka<br>Link: [hikari-kyoen.com](https://www.hikari-kyoen.com/en/midosuji/index.html) | Osaka's main boulevard strung with LED displays for about two months each winter, part of the citywide Osaka Hikari-Kyoen light festival. This visit catches opening night, 3 November. Free to walk, no ticket, evening hours only, on wide flat paved sidewalks - stroller-friendly, though opening night draws a crowd so keep to the outer edge of the path. |
| Culture Day (Bunka no Hi) | [28](#day-28---tuesday-november-3-2026---osaka) | National Museum of Art, Osaka, 4-2-55 Nakanoshima, Kita-ku, Osaka 530-0005<br>Link: [nmao.go.jp](https://www.nmao.go.jp/en/) | A national holiday, 3 November, on which the National Museum of Art, Osaka waives its usual roughly ¥430 adult admission - this is the one Osaka museum confirmed free that date, not a citywide policy, and separate from Kansai Culture Day around 15-16 November, which falls after the trip. The entire exhibition space is underground behind a cluster of curved steel entrance pylons; fully lift-served, no stairs-only stretches, closed Mondays. |

### Theatre and performance

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Kabukiza single-act (hitomakumi) | [2](#day-2---thursday-october-8-2026---tokyo) | Kabukiza, 4-12-15 Ginza, Chuo-ku, Tokyo 104-0061 (single-act entrance and box office, building's left side, 4th floor)<br>Link: [kabukiweb.net](https://www.kabukiweb.net/) | A hitomakumi ticket buys standing/bench-row admission to just one act of Kabuki's four-hour daily programme rather than the whole show, for a fraction of the price - the practical way to see this centuries-old, highly stylized theatre form (all-male cast, live shamisen, a raised walkway through the audience) without committing an entire evening to it. About ¥2,500 including a ¥1,000 refundable-deposit English subtitle tablet, which solves the language problem the Japanese-only earphone guide doesn't. Tickets are sold same-day only, cash only, at a separate entrance, no advance booking. |
| Tokyo Rockabilly Club dancers | [5](#day-5---sunday-october-11-2026---tokyo) | Yoyogi Park, Harajuku entrance plaza, Shibuya-ku, Tokyo (same plaza as the park entrance; exact address unverified) | A free, unticketed open-air gathering: crews of 1950s-styled greasers, leather jackets and pompadours, meet most Sunday afternoons at the plaza by Yoyogi Park's Harajuku entrance to dance to rock and roll off a portable sound system, a tradition running since the 1980s. There is no official site for it and no fixed schedule - it is weather-dependent and not guaranteed, so a cold or rainy Sunday can mean nobody turns up. Free to watch, viewable from the sidelines or a stroller, commonly starting early-to-mid afternoon. |

### Transport as an experience

| What | Day | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sumida River water bus | [4](#day-4---saturday-october-10-2026---tokyo) | Hamarikyu Pier, Chuo-ku, Tokyo, to Asakusa Pier, Taito-ku, Tokyo<br>Link: [suijobus.co.jp](https://www.suijobus.co.jp/en/) | A roughly 40-minute Tokyo Cruise boat ride under 13 bridges down the Sumida River, turning what would otherwise be a dull subway transfer into scenic downtime that lands a five-to-ten-minute walk from the hotel. ¥1,000/adult on top of the day's other transport. Reconfirm the route is running before relying on it - Tokyo Cruise's fleet has faced extended service suspensions, and Hamarikyu boarding specifically has been withdrawn in the past. |
| Enoden coastal railway | [7](#day-7---tuesday-october-13-2026---kamakura-day-trip) | Kamakura Station, Kamakura, Kanagawa (line runs to Fujisawa)<br>Link: [enoden.co.jp](https://www.enoden.co.jp/en/) | A single-track local line that hugs the Kamakura coastline, running past Yuigahama Beach close enough to see the surf from the window - the ride itself, not just the stops it serves, is the point. ¥800 for the "Noriorikun" 1-day unlimited pass, which covers the hops between the Great Buddha, Hase-dera and the beach. Boarding queues at Kamakura Station can run 30-45 minutes on a fine holiday weekend. |
| Kawaguchiko Tenjozan Ropeway | [12](#day-12---sunday-october-18-2026---kawaguchiko) | 1163 Azagawa, Fujikawaguchiko-machi, Minamitsuru-gun, Yamanashi 401-0303<br>Link: [mtfujiropeway.jp](https://www.mtfujiropeway.jp/en/) | A cable car that climbs about 400m up Mt. Tenjo in under three minutes to a summit deck with an unbroken panorama of Mt. Fuji across the full width of Lake Kawaguchi - worth the fare for the view alone, the single best elevated vantage point on the Kawaguchiko leg on a clear day. ¥1,000/adult round trip, infants free. Both stations are step-free and the cabin takes a folded stroller or a carrier-worn infant. |
| Lake Kawaguchi sightseeing cruise | [12](#day-12---sunday-october-18-2026---kawaguchiko) | Funatsuhama Pier, Kawaguchiko, ~10 min walk from Kawaguchiko Stn (street address unverified)<br>Link: [fujigokokisen.jp](https://www.fujigokokisen.jp/contents/en/) | A 20-minute loop around Lake Kawaguchi on the red sightseeing boat, passing under the Kawaguchiko Ohashi bridge and around Unoshima Island for a water-level angle on Mt. Fuji different from the park or the ropeway view. ¥1,200/adult, infants free. Boarding is via a short gangway with a handrail, not wheel-on, but a folded stroller can be carried aboard. |
